For every
\beta_{0}>0
and
k
there exists
\alpha_{0}>0
such that for all integers
n,k
,
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
,
m=\alpha n
, and
0\leq h\leq{km}
, we have

p(h,k,m,n)\leq\left(\frac{\beta_{0}h}{n}\right)^{h/2}\,.

Furthermore,
p(h,k,m,n)=0
if
h>km
.

We include a proof for convenience.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
\alpha_{0}=\beta_{0}/(8e^{3}k^{5})
.
The definition of
p(\cdots)
explores the probability that a fixed set
H=[h]
satisfies some conditions with respect to a random matching
M
. By symmetry we can instead view it as the probability that a uniformly random set
H
satisfies the same conditions with respect to a fixed matching
M
with edges
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
. (We abuse notation to also use
M
to denote
\cup_{i\in[m]}e_{i}
, i.e., the subset of vertices incident to the matching.)

Given a matching
M
, let
\mathcal{F}=\{H\subseteq[n]\mid|H|=h,H\subseteq M,|H\cap e_{i}|\neq 1\}
. We have
p(h,k,m,n)=|\mathcal{F}|/\binom{n}{h}
, and so it suffices to bound
|\mathcal{F}|
from above.
Given
H\in\mathcal{F}
, let
E(H)=\{i\in[m]|H\cap e_{i}\neq\emptyset\}

denote the set of edges touching
H
and let
\eta=|E(H)|
. We have that
h/k\leq\eta\leq h/2
since every edge includes at least two vertices of
H
. To choose an
H\in\mathcal{F}
we may choose
\eta\in[h/k,h/2]
,
E\subseteq[m]
of size
\eta
and then choose
H
of size
h
from the set of vertices incident to
E
. (There are further conditions that we will ignore to get the upper bound.) Given
\eta
there are
\binom{m}{\eta}
ways of choosing
E
, and given
E
, there are at most
\binom{k\eta}{h}
ways of choosing
H
from the vertices touched by
E
. We thus get that
|\mathcal{F}|\leq\sum_{\eta=h/k}^{h/2}\binom{m}{\eta}\binom{k\eta}{h}
. Applying this we now get the following inequalities:

\displaystyle p(h,k,m,n)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{n}{h}^{-1}\cdot\sum_{\eta=h/k}^{h/2}\binom{m}{\eta}\binom{k\eta}{h}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\eta=h/k}^{h/2}\binom{m}{\eta}(ek\eta/h)^{h}(h/n)^{h}\mbox{~~~~(Using $(a/b)^{b}\leq\binom{a}{b}\leq(ea/b)^{b}$)}

\displaystyle\leq(ekh/n)^{h}\sum_{\eta=1}^{h/2}\binom{m}{\eta}\mbox{~~~~(Using $\eta\leq h$)}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{h}(ekh/n)^{h}(2ekm/h)^{h/2}

\displaystyle=(8e^{3}k^{5}\alpha h/n)^{h/2}\,,

where the last inequality uses
\sum_{\eta=1}^{h/2}\binom{m}{\eta}\leq 2^{h}(2ekm/h)^{h/2}
for every
m
and
h\in[km]
. (If
h\leq m
the final term is the largest and bounded by
(2ekm/h)^{h/2}
and so the entire sum is at most
h(2ekm/h)^{h/2}\leq 2^{h}(2ekm/h)^{h/2}
. If
h\in(m,km]
, then the sum is at most
2^{m}
while the RHS is at least
2^{h}
(in particular
2ekm/h\geq 1
).) So we have that
p(h,k,m,n)\leq(8e^{3}k^{5}\alpha h/n)^{h/2}\leq(8e^{3}k^{5}\alpha_{0}h/n)^{h/2}=(\beta_{0}n/h)^{h/2}
since
\beta_{0}=8e^{3}k^{5}\alpha_{0}
.
∎

The following lemma is an immediate corollary of
Lemma

6.14
and will be useful later in the proof of
5.18
.

6.15
.

For every
k,q\geq 2
there exists
\alpha_{0}>0
such that for every
\delta\in(0,1/2)
and
C<\infty
there exists
\tau>0
such that for all integers
n
,
s
and
m
satisfying
4\log_{q}(3/\delta)\leq s\leq\tau n
,
m\leq\alpha_{0}n
we have:

p(h,k,m,n)U_{C,s}(h)\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta^{2h}&,\ 1\leq h\leq s\\
2^{-h/2}&,\ s<h\leq km\\
0&,\ h>km\end{array}\right.\,.

Specifically,

\sum_{h=2}^{n}p(h,k,m,n)U_{C,s}(h)\leq\delta^{2}\,.

Proof.

Let
\beta_{0}=\frac{1}{4q^{2}e^{2}}
, and let
\alpha_{0}
be as in
Lemma

6.14
for this choice of
\beta_{0}
. Let
\tau=\delta^{8}/(C^{2}\beta_{0}^{2})
.

By
Lemma

6.14
and the definition of (strongly-)boundedness we have the following:

•

If
1\leq h\leq s
, then

\displaystyle p(h,k,m,n)U_{C,s}(h)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\beta_{0}h}{n}\right)^{h/2}\cdot\left(\frac{C\sqrt{sn}}{h}\right)^{h/2}=\left(\frac{\beta_{0}C\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{h/2}\leq(\beta_{0}C\sqrt{\tau})^{h/2}\leq\delta^{2h},

where the second inequality uses
s\leq\tau n
and the third uses
\beta_{0}C\sqrt{\tau}\leq\delta^{4}
.

•

If
s<h\leq km
, then

\displaystyle p(h,k,m,n)U_{C,s}(h)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\beta_{0}h}{n}\right)^{h/2}\cdot\left(\frac{2q^{2}e^{2}n}{h}\right)^{h/2}=(\beta_{0}2q^{2}e^{2})^{h/2}\leq 2^{-h/2},

where the final inequality uses
\beta_{0}2q^{2}e^{2}\leq 1/2
.

•

If
h>km
, we have
p(h,k,m,n)=0
and hence
p(h,k,m,n)U_{C,s}(h)=0
.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=2}^{n}p(h,k,m,n)U_{C,s}(h)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=2}^{s}\delta^{2h}+\sum_{h=s+1}^{km}2^{-h/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta^{4}}{1-\delta^{2}}+\frac{2^{-s-1/2}}{1-(1/\sqrt{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}+\frac{2^{-s-1/2}}{1-(1/\sqrt{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2},

where the second inequality uses
\delta<1/2
and the third uses
s\geq 4\log_{q}(3/\delta)
.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove the main lemma of this subsection.

Proof of
Lemma

5.18
.

Given
k,q\geq 2
and
\delta>0
and
C<\infty
let
\alpha_{0}=\alpha_{0}(k,q)>0
and
\tau=\tau(k,q,\delta,C)>0
be as given by
Lemma

6.15
.

Let
m\leq\alpha_{0}n
,
4\log_{q}(3/\delta)\leq b\leq s\leq\tau n
, and let
B\subset\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
be a
(C,s)
-bounded set with
|B|\geq q^{n-b}
. The goal is to prove that with probability at least
1-\delta
over a uniform random choice of
k
-hypermatching
M
on
m
edges and a random choice of center sequence
\mathbf{c}
the following holds for every
\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}
,

1-\delta\leq q^{(k-1)m}\Pr_{\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)}[A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=-\mathbf{z}_{0}]\leq 1+\delta

(Recall that
A_{\mathbf{c}}
was defined in
Eq.

3.3
. Note that the switch from
\mathbf{z}_{0}
to
-\mathbf{z}_{0}
in the event described above does not alter the statement being proved since we are proving this for every vector
\mathbf{z}_{0}
.)

Now, for a fixed
k
-hypermatching
M
and fixed choice of centers
\mathbf{c}
, let us expand the marginal probability as follows. Let
f:\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
be the indicator function of the set
B
. For a fixed
\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}
, let
g=g_{A_{\mathbf{c}},\mathbf{z}_{0}}:\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
be the function given by
g(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{1}_{A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{z}_{0}}
. Letting
g=g_{A_{\mathbf{c}},\mathbf{z}_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle q^{(k-1)m}\Pr_{\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)}[A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=-\mathbf{z}_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{(k-1)m}}{|B|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}}f(\mathbf{x})g(-\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{(k-1)m}}{|B|}(f\star g)(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{(k-1)m}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\widehat{f\star g}(\mathbf{u})

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{(k-1)m+n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})~~~\mbox{(By \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:convthm})}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{q^{n+(k-1)m}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\neq 0^{n}\end{subarray}}\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})

(6.16)

(Since
q^{n}\widehat{f}(0)=|B|
and
q^{n}\widehat{g}(0)=q^{n-(k-1)m}
).

We now analyze the Fourier coefficients of
g
and use this to bound the right hand side above. Roughly the claim below establishes basic properties of the function
g
that show that
g
is also a somewhat reduced function (as in
Definition

5.16
). This, combined with the boundedness of
B
allows us to establish the near-uniformity of the posterior distribution.

6.17
.

Let
M
be a
k
-hypermatching of size
m
,
\mathbf{c}
be centers, and
\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}
. Let
g(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{1}_{A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{z}_{0}}
. For every
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
, the following conditions hold:

1.

If
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\not\subseteq\textsf{supp}(M)
then
\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})=0
.
11
11

11

Recall that
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})=\{i|u_{i}\neq 0\}
and
\textsf{supp}(M)
is the subset of [n] consisting of vertices that are incident to some hyperedge in the matching
M
.

2.

If there exists
i\in[m]
such that
\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle\not\equiv 0
mod
q
where
\mathbf{e}_{i}
denotes the
i
-th hyperedge of
M
, then
\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})=0
.

3.

|\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})|\leq q^{-(k-1)m}
.

Proof of
6.17
.

Recall that

q^{n}\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})=\sum_{\mathbf{x}}g(\mathbf{x})\omega^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{x}}
.

1.

If
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\not\subseteq\textsf{supp}(M)
, then there exists
i\in[n]
such that
u_{i}\neq 0
but the
i
-th column of
A_{\mathbf{c}}
is zero. For each
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
, for every
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
we have
g(\mathbf{x})=g(\mathbf{x}+a\boldsymbol{\delta}_{i})
, where
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
denotes the coordinate vector in the
i
-th direction (i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{i}=0^{i-1}10^{n-i}
). Also, note that
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}}\omega^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\mathbf{x}+a\boldsymbol{\delta}_{i})}=\omega^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{x}}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}}\omega^{u_{i}\cdot a}=0
. This implies
\hat{g}(\mathbf{u})=0
.

2.

Suppose
\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle\not\equiv 0
mod
q
. For each
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
, note that
g(\mathbf{x})=g(\mathbf{x}+a\mathbf{e}_{i})
because
a^{k}
lies in the kernel of the folded matrix of this hyperedge. Second, since
\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle\not\equiv 0
mod
q
, we have
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}}\omega^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{x}+a\mathbf{e}_{i}}=\omega^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{x}}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}}\omega^{a\cdot\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle}=0
. This implies
\hat{g}(\mathbf{u})=0
.

3.

By definition, we have
q^{n}\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})=\sum_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=z_{0}}\omega^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{x}}
. Note that for fixed
M,\mathbf{c},z_{0}
, there are at most
q^{n-(k-1)m}

\mathbf{x}
such that
g(\mathbf{x})=1
. Thus, we have
|\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})|\leq q^{-(k-1)m}
as desired.

∎

Now, we can use
6.17
to further upper bound
Equation 6.16
as follows. Recall that
\odot
stands for the coordinate-wise product of vectors.

\frac{q^{n+(k-1)m}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\neq 0^{n}\end{subarray}}\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})\widehat{g}(\mathbf{u})\leq\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\neq 0^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\text{ is matched by }M\\
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{1}\equiv 0\text{ mod }q\ \forall i\in[m]\end{subarray}}|\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})|\,.

One key observation here is that the above bound is independent of
\mathbf{z}_{0}
and therefore holds even if we take the maximum of the left hand side over all
\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}
. We thus get, for every
M
and
\mathbf{c}
:

\max_{\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}}\left|q^{(k-1)m}\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)\end{subarray}}[A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=-\mathbf{z}_{0}]-1\right|\leq\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\neq 0^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\text{ is contained in }M\\
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{1}\equiv 0\text{ mod }q\ \forall i\in[m]\end{subarray}}|\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})|\,

Finally, let us take the expectation of the above quantity over the randomness of
M
and
\mathbf{c}
.

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M,\mathbf{c}}\left[\max_{\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}}\left|q^{(k-1)m}\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)\end{subarray}}[A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=-\mathbf{z}_{0}]-1\right|\right]\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M,\mathbf{c}}\left[\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\neq 0^{n}\\
\mathbf{u}\text{ is contained in }M\\
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{1}\equiv 0\text{ mod }q\ \forall i\in[m]\end{subarray}}|\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})|\right]\,.

Next, we partition the summation according to the
\ell_{0}
-norm of the Fourier coefficients.

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{n}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M,\mathbf{c}}\left[\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{0}=h\\
\mathbf{u}\text{ is contained in }M\\
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{1}\equiv 0\text{ mod }q\ \forall i\in[m]\end{subarray}}|\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})|\right]\,

Observe that the event
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{1}\equiv 0\pmod{q}
implies that either
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{0}=0
or
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{0}\geq 2
holds. Hence, the above summation can be replaced with a summation beginning at
h=2
, and the equation becomes

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=2}^{n}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M,\mathbf{c}}\left[\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{0}=h\\
\mathbf{u}\text{ is contained in }M\\
\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{0}=0\text{ or }\|\mathbf{u}\odot\mathbf{e}_{i}\|_{0}\geq 2\ \forall i\in[m]\end{subarray}}|\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})|\right]\leq\sum_{h=2}^{n}p(h,k,m,n)\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}|\widehat{f}(\mathbf{u})|\,

When
B
is
(C,s)
-bounded, we can further upper bound the above quantity as follows.

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=2}^{n}p(h,k,m,n)\cdot U_{C,s}(h)\leq\delta^{2},

where the last inequality is due to
6.15
. Thus, when
\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)
and
U\sim\textsf{Unif}(\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)\alpha n})
, we have

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M,\mathbf{c}}\left[\max_{\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}}\left|q^{(k-1)m}\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)\end{subarray}}[A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=-\mathbf{z}_{0}]-1\right|\right]\leq\delta^{2}\,.

By Markov’s inequality, we have

\max_{\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{(k-1)m}}\left|q^{(k-1)m}\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\textsf{Unif}(B)\end{subarray}}[A_{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{x}=-\mathbf{z}_{0}]-1\right|\leq\delta

with probability at least
1-\delta
. This yields the main part of the lemma. The consequences follow directly from the main part (since pointwise bounds on the distance between distributions imply total variation distance as well as expectation of a non-negative weight).

This completes the proof of
Lemma

5.18
.
∎

6.5
Proof of the “induction step” lemma

The goal of this section is to prove the “induction step” lemma. By Markov’s inequality, it suffices to prove the following lemma which is the expectation version of
Lemma

5.19
. We first show how
Lemma

6.18
implies
Lemma

5.19
and then focus on proving the former in the rest of this subsection.

6.18
Induction step in expectation.

For every
q,k\in\mathbb{N}
there exist
\alpha_{0}>0
and
C_{0}>0
such that for every
C>C_{0}
, there exist
\tau_{0}\in(0,1)
and
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that the following holds:
For every
n,m,s,h\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying
m\leq\alpha_{0}n
and
0<s<\tau_{0}n
and
1\leq h\leq s
, and every
B\subset\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
that
is
(C,s)
-strongly-bounded we have:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M}\left[\max_{B^{\prime}}\left\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|\right\}\right]\leq W_{C^{\prime\prime},s}(h)\,,

where the expectation is taken over a uniform random
k
-hypermatching
M
on
m
hyperedges, and the maximum is taken over all
B^{\prime}
that are
(M,C_{0},s)
-reduced.

We first restate and prove
Lemma

5.19
using
Lemma

6.18
.
See
5.19

Proof of
Lemma

5.19
.

Let
\alpha_{0}
,
C_{0}
be as in
Lemma

6.18
. Given
\alpha,C
and
\delta
, let
C^{\prime\prime}
and
\tau_{0}
be the constants given by

Lemma

6.18
. Let
C^{\prime}
be the constant from
Lemma

6.5
for
C=C^{\prime\prime}/\delta^{2}
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\tau_{0}
.
We prove our lemma with these choices of parameters.

For every matching
M
, fix a set
B^{\prime}=B^{\prime}(M)
that is
(M,C_{0},s)
-reduced and satisfies
|B^{\prime}|\geq q^{n-b^{\prime}}
and
|B\cap B^{\prime}|\geq(1-\delta)\cdot|B|\cdot|B^{\prime}|/q^{n}\geq q^{n-s}
. We prove the lemma for every such fixing. (In particular
B^{\prime}
below is short for
B^{\prime}(M)
.)

Fix
s\leq\tau_{0}n
.
For every
h\in\{1,\dots,s\}
, by the convolution theorem (see
Lemma

2.9
) for Fourier coefficients, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B\cap B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B\cap B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{v})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B\cap B^{\prime}|}\left|\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}}\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B\cap B^{\prime}|}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\cdot\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{u}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B\cap B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\cdot\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{|B|\cdot|B^{\prime}|}{q^{n}\cdot|B\cap B^{\prime}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|\,,

where the first inequality above is from the triangle inequality and the second from the assumption in the lemma statement on the cardinality of
B\cap B^{\prime}
.

For
h\in[s]
, let
F(h)
denote the event that the random matching
M
is such that

\frac{1}{1-\delta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|>\frac{1}{\delta^{h}}\cdot W_{C^{\prime\prime},s}(h)=W_{C^{\prime\prime}/\delta^{2},s}(h).

Further, for
h\in(s,\tau_{0}n]
, let
F(h)
denote the event that the random matching
M
is such that

\frac{1}{1-\delta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|>\frac{1}{\delta^{h}}\cdot W_{C^{\prime\prime},h}(h)=W_{C^{\prime\prime}/\delta^{2},h}(h).

(6.19)

Let
F=\cup_{h\in[\tau_{0}n]}F(h)
be the union of these events. Note that if
F
does not hold, then, for every
s^{\prime}\in[s,\tau_{0}n]
,
B\cap B^{\prime}
is
(C^{\prime\prime}/\delta^{2},s^{\prime})
-weakly-bounded, and so, by
Lemma

6.5
,
B\cap B^{\prime}
is
(C^{\prime},s)
-strongly-bounded as desired. So we turn to bounding the probability of
F
.

For
h\in[s]
, an application of Markov’s inequality to
Lemma

6.18
yields that

\Pr[F(h)]\leq\frac{W_{C^{\prime\prime},s}(h)}{(1-\delta)\cdot W_{C^{\prime\prime}/\delta^{2},s}(h)}=\frac{W_{C^{\prime\prime},s}(h)}{(1-\delta)\cdot\frac{1}{\delta^{h}}W_{C^{\prime\prime},s}(h)}\leq\frac{\delta^{h}}{1-\delta}.

For
h\in(s,\tau_{0}n]
, we first note that since
B
is
(C,s)
-bounded then it is also
(C,h)
-bounded (by Item (2) of
Lemma

6.4
). Similarly we also have that
B^{\prime}
is
(M,C_{0},h)
-reduced. This allows us to invoke
Lemma

6.18
with
s_{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:induction step exp}}=h
and then proceed as in the case above. Specifically for this choice of
s_{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:induction step exp}}
we get by
Lemma

6.18
that the expected value of the LHS of
Eq.

6.19
is at most
W_{C^{\prime\prime},h}(h)/(1-\delta)
. Now an application of Markov’s inequality yields:

\Pr[F(h)]\leq\frac{W_{C^{\prime\prime},h}(h)}{(1-\delta)\cdot W_{C^{\prime\prime}/\delta^{2},h}(h)}=\frac{W_{C^{\prime\prime},h}(h)}{(1-\delta)\cdot\frac{1}{\delta^{h}}W_{C^{\prime\prime},h}(h)}\leq\frac{\delta^{h}}{1-\delta}\,.

We thus get

\Pr[F]\leq\sum_{h\in[\tau_{0}n]}F(h)\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\sum_{h}\delta^{h}\leq 4\delta
where the final step uses the fact that
\delta<1/2
. We conclude that with probability at least
1-4\delta
over the randomness of
M
, the event
F
does not hold and
B\cap B^{\prime}
is
(C^{\prime},s)
-bounded.
∎

Now we turn to proving
Lemma

6.18
. The proof involves three steps. In the first step we partition the inner sum over
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
based on a combinatorial structure that allows us to say how much the expected contribution of
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
would be, based on a few parameters. In the second step we give bounds on these expected contributions in different cases and analyze the probability of each case. In the final step we then combine these different bounds to prove the lemma.

Step 1: Partitioning the inner sum via a combinatorial structure.

We start by defining the following combinatorial quantity, based on intersection properties of a random
k
-hypermatching.

6.20
.

Let
n,q,k,u\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in(0,1/k)
. Let
\mathbf{u}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{q}\backslash\{0\})^{u}\times 0^{n-u}
be a vector that is non-zero on exactly the first
u
coordinates. For a
k
-hypermatching
M
of size
m
, let
K_{\mathbf{u}}(M):=\{i\in[m]\,|\,\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle\not\equiv 0\pmod{q}\}
be the set of edges with “odd intersection” (formally non-zero inner product mod
q
) with
\mathbf{u}
.
Let
E_{\mathbf{u}}(M):=\{j\in[n]\,\mid\,u_{j}\neq 0,\,\exists i\not\in K_{\mathbf{u}}(M),\ j\in e_{i}\}
denote the set of vertices in the support of
\mathbf{u}
that are in ‘‘even’’ edges.
12
12

12

Informally we refer to edges as “even” (or “odd”) which would be the right terminology if
q=2
. For
q\neq 2
these words are formalized as having zero (or non-zero) inner product with
\mathbf{u}
.
Finally, let
O_{\mathbf{u}}(M):=\{j\in[n]\,\mid\,u_{j}\neq 0,\,\exists i\in K_{\mathbf{u}}(M),\ j\in e_{i}\}
be the vertices in the support of
\mathbf{u}
from odd edges. For
o,\eta,\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, we define

p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa):=\max_{\mathbf{u}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{q}\backslash\{0\})^{u}\times 0^{n-u}}\Pr_{M}\left[|K_{\mathbf{u}}(M)|=\kappa,|E_{\mathbf{u}}(M)|=\eta,|O_{\mathbf{u}}(M)|=o\right],

(6.21)

where
M
is a uniformly random
k
-hypermatching of size
\alpha n
. (In other words
p_{q,\alpha}
is the maximum probability of a vector
\mathbf{u}
of support size
u
having
\kappa
odd edges,
\eta
even vertices and
o
odd vertices when the matching
M
is drawn at random.)

Fig.

4
illustrates some of the parameters in the definition above.
We remark that
p_{q,\alpha}(\cdots)
should not be confused with the function
p(\cdots)
defined in
Definition

6.13
, which is a similar combinatorial quantity but not the same.

Note that as each edge in
K_{\mathbf{u}}(M)
contributes at least one element to
O_{\mathbf{u}}(M)
, we have
o\geq\kappa
.

Figure 4:
A graphical intuition for the parameters appeared in
Definition

6.20
.

We now show how to bound a certain expected value of the sum of Fourier coefficients of a fixed “level” from above in terms of the combinatorial quantity defined in
6.20
.

6.22
.

Let
n,q,k,u\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in(0,1/k)
,
0\leq s\leq n
, and
C>0
.
For every
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
with
u=|\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})|
and
h\in[s]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M}\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}B^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\text{$B^{\prime}$ is $(M,C,s)$-reduced}\end{subarray}}\left\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|\right\}\right]

\displaystyle~~~\leq\ \sum_{o,\eta,\kappa}p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)\cdot(h+1)\cdot q^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C,s}(h+o+\eta-(u+\kappa))\,.

Furthermore, if
p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)>0
then (1)
u\geq\eta+o
, (2)
\kappa\leq o\leq k\cdot\kappa
and (3)
h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa)\geq 0
.

Proof.

As suggested by the right hand side, we consider the various possibilities for
o,\eta,\kappa
and bound the left hand side conditioned on the event defined by the probability (
6.21
).

Let
u=|\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})|
. Consider a fixed matching
M=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}\}
with
m=\alpha n
and
|K_{\mathbf{u}}(M)|=\kappa
,
|E_{\mathbf{u}}(M)|=\eta
, and
|O_{\mathbf{u}}(M)|=o
(see the relevant definitions in
Definition

6.20
). Given
M
, let
A=\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\setminus(E_{\mathbf{u}}(M)\cup O_{\mathbf{u}}(M))
be the set of unmatched vertices of
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})
. Furthermore, let
a=|A|
, so that
a=u-(\eta+o)
. For ease of notation, we drop the dependence on
\mathbf{u}
and
M
and simply write
E=E_{\mathbf{u}}(M)
and
O=O_{\mathbf{u}}(M)
. We also abuse notation and often use
M
to denote the subset of
[n]
given by
\cup_{i\in[m]}e_{i}
. (The distinction is hopefully clear from context.)

Note that since
|A|\geq 0
we must have
u-(\eta+o)\geq 0
for such a matching to exist. This establishes condition (1) of the “Furthermore” part of the lemma. Note further that each edge in
K_{\mathbf{u}}(M)
contributes at least one vertex, and at most
k
vertices, to
O
and so
\kappa\leq o\leq k\cdot\kappa
establishing condition (2). We now proceed to proving the rest of the lemma (and will prove condition (3) along the way).

Let
B^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
be an
(M,C,s)
-reduced set. We give an upper bound on

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|

in terms of the parameters
o,\eta,\kappa
, which will suffice to establish the lemma. We start by establishing some conditions that are necessary to get
\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\neq 0
.

We start with some more notation: For a set
S\subseteq[n]
, we define the restriction of
\mathbf{u}
with respect to
S
to be the vector
\mathbf{u}|_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
where
(\mathbf{u}|_{S})_{j}=\mathbf{u}_{j}
if
j\in S
; otherwise
(\mathbf{u}|_{S})_{j}=0
. We define the closure of
S
(with respect to the matching
M
) to be the set
\overline{S}=\cup_{\{i\in[m]\mid S\cap e_{i}\neq\emptyset\}}e_{i}
, i.e.,
\overline{S}
takes all the vertices that are contained in edges that touch
S
. (We only apply the notion of the closure to sets
S\subseteq M
.)

6.23
.

For every
\mathbf{u}
there exists a vector
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}
such that for every vector
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
satisfying
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h
, we have
\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\neq 0
only if there exists
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{q}
with
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{z})\subseteq\overline{O}
and

\tau=\tau(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\in[\kappa,h-a]
such that
\|\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+(\mathbf{z}+\tilde{\mathbf{u}})\|_{0}=h-a-\tau
. In particular,
\kappa\leq h-a
.

Before proving the claim we note that the claim implies condition (3) of the “Furthermore” part of the lemma since
\kappa\leq h-a
and
a=u-\eta-o
imply together that
h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa)=h-a-\kappa\geq 0
. We now prove the claim.

Proof.

We prove the claim for
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}:=\mathbf{u}|_{[n]\setminus A}
.
Note that for every vector
\mathbf{v}
and set
S\subseteq[n]
we can write
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}|_{S}+\mathbf{v}_{[n]\setminus S}
, and we also have
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{0}=\|\mathbf{v}|_{S}\|_{0}+\|\mathbf{v}_{[n]\setminus S}\|_{0}
. We use this to decompose
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}|_{A}+\tilde{\mathbf{u}}
.

Now consider
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
such that
\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\neq 0
and
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h
. First, as
B^{\prime}
is
(M,C,s)
-reduced, by
Lemma

6.1
we have
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\subseteq M
.
Again we write
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{A}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{[n]\setminus A}
. Since
A\cap M=\emptyset
we must have
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{A}=0
. Thus we get that
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}|_{[n]\setminus A}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{[n]\setminus A}
and so

\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=\|\mathbf{u}|_{[n]\setminus A}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{[n]\setminus A}\|_{0}=\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}+\|\mathbf{u}|_{A}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{A}\|_{0}=h-a,

where the final equality uses
\|\mathbf{u}|_{A}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{A}\|_{0}=\|\mathbf{u}|_{A}\|_{0}
which equals
a
since
A\subseteq\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})
.

We show now that for
\mathbf{z}:=-(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|_{\overline{O}}

and
\tau:=\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}|_{\overline{O}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{\overline{O}}\|_{0}
, we have
\|(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{z})+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h-a-\tau
.

Note that the definition of
\mathbf{z}
is such that we have
(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|_{\overline{O}}=0
. This ensures

\|(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{z})+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}|_{[n]\setminus\overline{O}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{[n]\setminus\overline{O}}\|_{0}=\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}-\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}|_{\overline{O}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{\overline{O}}\|_{0}=h-a-\tau\,.

Finally, we would like to bound the range of possible values for
\tau
. For the upper bound, we have

\tau:=\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}|_{\overline{O}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{\overline{O}}\|_{0}\leq\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h-a.

For the lower bound we for claim that
\|\mathbf{u}|_{e_{i}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{e_{i}}\|_{0}\geq 1
for every edge
e_{i}
with
i\in K
. This is so since
\langle\mathbf{u},e_{i}\rangle\neq 0
(definition of
K
) and
\langle\mathbf{u}^{\prime},e_{i}\rangle=0
(since
\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\neq 0
), and together they imply
\langle\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime},e_{i}\rangle\neq 0
which can only happen if
(\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|_{e_{i}}\neq 0
, which in turn implies
\|\mathbf{u}|_{e_{i}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{e_{i}}\|_{0}=\|(\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|_{e_{i}}\|_{0}\geq 1
.

From the above claim it follows that

\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}|_{\overline{O}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{\overline{O}}\|_{0}=\|\mathbf{u}|_{\overline{O}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{\overline{O}}\|_{0}=\sum_{i\in K}\|\mathbf{u}|_{e_{i}}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|_{e_{i}}\|_{0}\geq\sum_{i\in K}1=\kappa.

This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

We now return to analyzing the summation in the LHS of the lemma statement. Let
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}
be as given by
6.23
.
We have:

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau=\kappa}^{h-a}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{z})\subseteq\overline{O}\end{subarray}}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\mathbf{z}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=\mathbf{u},\tau(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=\tau\\
\|(\mathbf{z}+\tilde{\mathbf{u}})+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h-a-\tau\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|\right]

(Using
6.23
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau=\kappa}^{h-a}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{z})\subseteq\overline{O}\end{subarray}}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|(\mathbf{z}+\tilde{\mathbf{u}})+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h-a-\tau\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau=\kappa}^{h-a}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{z})\subseteq\overline{O}\end{subarray}}U_{C,s}(h-a-\tau)

(Using the
(C,s)
-reducedness of
B^{\prime}
with respect to the vector
\mathbf{v}:=\tilde{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{z}
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau=\kappa}^{h-a}q^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C,s}(h-a-\tau)

(Using
|\overline{O}|=k\kappa
to get
|\{\mathbf{z}\,|\,\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{z})\subseteq\overline{O}\}|\leq q^{k\kappa}
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau=\kappa}^{h-a}q^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C,s}(h-a-\kappa)

(Using monotonicity of
U_{C,s}(h)
when
h\in[s]
by
Lemma

6.4
)

\displaystyle=(h-a-\kappa+1)\cdot q^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C,s}(h-a-\kappa)

\displaystyle\leq(h+1)\cdot q^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C,s}(h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa)).

This proves the lemma. ∎

Step 2: Useful inequalities about the boundedness parameters and the combinatorial structure.

In order to quantify the upper bound in
6.22
, we need to obtain an upper bound for the combinatorial quantity
p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)
.

6.24
.

For every
q,k\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C
such that for every
\alpha\in(0,1/k]
and every
n,u,\kappa,o,\eta\in\mathbb{N}
we have:

p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)\leq\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot C^{u}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\,.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
C=2qe^{3}k
.
We start by establishing some (significant amount of) notation for the proof. The proof consists of two steps: (i) upper bounding
p_{q,\alpha}(\cdots)
by
\sum_{d=\eta/\kappa}^{\eta/2}N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)/\binom{n}{u}
where
N_{q}(\cdots)
is a certain well-defined combinatorial quantity and (ii) upper bounding
N_{q}(\cdots)
.

Step (i) of the proof for
Lemma

6.24
.

For
\mathbf{u}=(\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
, let
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\subseteq[n]
denote the subset of non-zero coordinates of
\mathbf{u}
. Further, for
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
, let
\textsf{supp}_{i}(\mathbf{u})
denote the subset
\{j\in[n]\mid\ \mathbf{u}_{j}=i\}
.
Now given non-negative integers
u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}
and
u=u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}
, let
S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}=\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\mid|\textsf{supp}_{i}(\mathbf{u})|=u_{i}\,\forall i\in[q-1]\}
and let
S_{u}=\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\mid|\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})|=u\}
.

Given a vector
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
and hypermatching
M
containing
m=\alpha n
hyperedges
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
where each
e_{i}
is viewed as a subset of
[n]
of size
k
, we define four associated sets below. Let:

•

K=\{i\in[m]\mid\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle\not\equiv 0\pmod{q}\}
,

•

O=\{j\in\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\mid\exists i\in K,j\in e_{i}\}
,

•

E=\{j\in\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\mid\exists i\in[m]\setminus K,j\in e_{i}\}
, and

•

D=\{i\in[m]:E\cap e_{i}\neq\emptyset\}.

Note that
p_{q,\alpha}(\cdots)
bounds the maximum over
\mathbf{u}
with
|\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})|=u
of the probability, over a random hypermatching
M
, that
|K|=\kappa
,
|O|=o
and
|E|=\eta
. By symmetry however we can fix the matching
M
and consider the maximum, over
u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}
s.t.
u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u
, of the probability that
|K|=\kappa
,
|O|=o
and
|E|=\eta
, when
\mathbf{u}
is chosen uniformly from
S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}
. In notation, we have

p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)=\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)]\right\}

where
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)
is the event that
|K|=\kappa
,
|O|=o
, and
|E|=\eta
.
Now let
\mathcal{E}_{d}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)
denote the event that
|K|=\kappa
,
|O|=0
,
|E|=\eta
, and
|D|=d
. Note that each hyperedge in
D
contributes at least two elements to
E
(since
\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e}_{i}\rangle\equiv 0\pmod{q}
for
i\in D
). Hence,
d\leq\eta/2
. Moreover, as each edge in
D
can contribute at most
k
elements to
E
, we also have
d\geq\eta/k
. Thus we get:

\displaystyle p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathcal{E}_{d}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathcal{E}_{d}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)]\right\}\right)\,.

Define
T^{+}((u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}),d,o,\eta,\kappa)
to be the set
\{\mathbf{u}_{0}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}\mid|K|=\kappa,|O|=o,|E|=\eta,|D|=d\}
,
let
T_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)=\cup_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}T^{+}((u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}),d,o,\eta,\kappa)
. Intuitively,
T_{q}
is the set that contains all the possible
\mathbf{u}_{0}
in the event
\mathcal{E}_{d}
while
T^{+}
forms a partition for
T_{q}
. For every
u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}
we have

\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathcal{E}_{d}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)]=\sum_{\mathbf{u}_{0}\in T^{+}((u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}),d,o,\eta,\kappa)}\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{0}].

The final probability above
\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{0}]
is upper bounded by
1/\binom{n}{u}
. (
\mathbf{u}
is chosen by picking disjoint sets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{q-1}
uniformly subject to
|U_{i}|=u_{i}
. The event
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{0}
holds iff
U_{i}=\textsf{supp}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{0})
which in turn happens only if

\cup_{i}U_{i}=\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u}_{0})
which in turn happens with probability
1/\binom{n}{u}
.) Finally let
N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)=|T_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)|
.
We thus have

\displaystyle p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathcal{E}_{d}(\mathbf{u},o,\eta,\kappa)]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{\sum_{\mathbf{u}_{0}\in T^{+}((u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}),d,o,\eta,\kappa)}\Pr_{\mathbf{u}\in S_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}}}[\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{0}].\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{|T^{+}((u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}),d,o,\eta,\kappa)|\cdot\frac{1}{\binom{n}{u}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\max_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{q-1}\mid u_{1}+\cdots+u_{q-1}=u\}}\left\{N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)\cdot\frac{1}{\binom{n}{u}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\frac{N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)}{\binom{n}{u}}\,.

(6.25)

Thus to upper bound
p_{q,\alpha}(\cdots)
it suffices to upper bound
N_{q}(\cdots)
.

Step (ii) of the proof for
Lemma

6.24
.

A vector
\mathbf{u}\in T_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)
can be specified by specifying the sets
O
,
E
,
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})-(O\cup E)
, and then by specifying
\mathbf{u}|_{\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})}
i.e., the restriction of
\mathbf{u}
to
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})
. There are
(q-1)^{u}
choices of
\mathbf{u}|_{\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})}
. So we turn to counting the number of possible
O
’s and
E
’s.
O
may be specified by first specifying
K
and then selecting
O
from
\cup_{i\in K}e_{i}
. (There are further restrictions on the choices of
O
which we will ignore to get an upper bound.) There are
\binom{m}{\kappa}
choices of
K
and at most
\binom{k\kappa}{o}
choices of
O
given
K
. Similarly for
E
we have at most
\binom{m}{d}
choices of
D
and then at most
\binom{kd}{\eta}
choices of
E
given
D
.
Finally, there are at most
\binom{n-km}{u-o-\eta}
choices of
\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})\setminus(O\cup E)
, since they must be a set of
u-o-\eta
vertices outside the
m
edges of our hypermatching. Putting all this together we get the following upper bound on
N_{q}(\cdots)
:

N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)\leq\binom{\alpha n}{\kappa}\binom{\alpha n}{d}\binom{k\kappa}{o}\binom{kd}{\eta}\binom{n(1-\alpha k)}{u-o-\eta}(q-1)^{u}.

Using the bounds
\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{b}\leq\binom{a}{b}\leq\left(\frac{ea}{b}\right)^{b}
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)}{\binom{n}{u}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{e\alpha n}{\kappa}\right)^{\kappa}\left(\frac{e\alpha n}{d}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{ek\kappa}{o}\right)^{o}\left(\frac{ekd}{\eta}\right)^{\eta}\frac{(n(1-\alpha k))^{u-o-\eta}}{(u-o-\eta)!}(q-1)^{u}\cdot\left(\frac{u}{n}\right)^{u}

\displaystyle\leq n^{\kappa+d-\eta-o}\kappa^{-\kappa}d^{-d}\frac{u^{u}}{(u-o-\eta)!}\cdot\left(\alpha^{\kappa+d}e^{\kappa+d+o+\eta}k^{o+\eta}(q-1)^{u}\cdot\left(\frac{\kappa}{o}\right)^{o}\left(\frac{d}{\eta}\right)^{\eta}(1-k\alpha)^{u-o-\eta}\right)\,.

Recall from
Lemma

6.22
and step (i) of the proof that
\kappa\leq o
,
2d\leq\eta
, and
o+\eta\leq u
. Hence, we have that
0\leq\kappa/o,d/\eta\leq 1
and
e^{\kappa+d+o+\eta}k^{o+\eta}(q-1)^{u}\leq e^{2u}\cdot((q-1)k)^{u}\leq(qe^{2}k)^{u}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{u^{u-o-\eta}}{(u-o-\eta)!}\leq e^{u}.

Therefore, letting
C_{k}=qe^{3}k
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{N_{q}(u,d,o,\eta,\kappa)}{\binom{n}{u}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}^{u}\alpha^{\kappa+d}\cdot n^{\kappa+d-\eta-o}\kappa^{-\kappa}d^{-d}u^{o+\eta}=\left(\frac{\alpha n}{d}\right)^{d}\cdot C_{k}^{u}\alpha^{\kappa}\cdot n^{\kappa-\eta-o}\kappa^{-\kappa}u^{o+\eta}\,.

Hence, by (
6.25
), we have that for
C=2C_{k}=2qe^{3}k
,

\displaystyle p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}^{u}\alpha^{\kappa}\cdot n^{\kappa-\eta-o}\kappa^{-\kappa}u^{o+\eta}\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\frac{\alpha n}{d}\right)^{d}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}^{u}\alpha^{\kappa+\frac{\eta}{k}}\cdot n^{\kappa-\eta-o}\kappa^{-\kappa}u^{o+\eta}\sum_{d=\eta/k}^{\eta/2}\left(\frac{n}{d}\right)^{d}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}^{u}\alpha^{\frac{\eta+o}{k}}\cdot n^{\kappa-\eta-o}\kappa^{-\kappa}u^{o+\eta}\cdot\frac{\eta}{2}\left(\frac{2n}{\eta}\right)^{\eta/2}\mbox{~~~(Using $o\leq k\cdot\kappa$ from \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:mcssum})}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\frac{\eta+o}{k}}\cdot C^{u}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o},

where the second-to-last inequality follows from the fact that
n/d\geq e
and
x^{1/x}
is a decreasing function of
x
on
x\in(e,\infty)
. This completes the proof of
6.24
.
∎

Finally, we prove an additional inequality about the boundedness parameters. This will simplify the final proof of
6.18
.

6.26
.

For every
q,k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1/k)
so that the following holds. For every
C_{1},C_{2}>0
there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C_{3}>0
such that for every
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
and
s,n,u,h,\eta,o,\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
with
s=\varepsilon n\leq\varepsilon_{0}n
and
h\in[s]
and
u\in[n]
, we have

U_{C_{1},s}(u)\cdot p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C_{2},s}(h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa))\leq 4^{-u-2}W_{C_{3},s}(h).

Proof.

Given
q
and
k
,
let
C
be the constant from
6.24
.
Let
C_{0}=\sqrt{2}\cdot 4e\cdot q\cdot C\cdot 2^{k}
, and
let
\alpha_{0}=1/(e^{2}C_{0})^{k}
.
Now given
C_{1},C_{2}
, let
C_{4}=4\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}\cdot C\cdot 2^{k}
,
C_{5}=\max\{1,2\sqrt{C_{2}},2C_{2}\}
and

C_{6}=eC_{5}

(where
e
is the base of the natural logarithm).
Now let
\varepsilon_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{(e^{2}C_{4})^{4}},\frac{1}{(2e^{2}C_{4})^{4}},(e^{-2}/C_{4})^{16}\right\}

and
C_{3}=\max\{(16e^{3}C_{4}C_{6})^{2},(16C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{2},(32C_{6}/\alpha_{0}^{1/k})^{2},256C_{6}^{2},(16C_{6}/\alpha_{0}^{1/k})^{2}\}
.
We prove the lemma for this choice of
\alpha_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
C_{3}
.
Note in particular that this choice of
\alpha_{0}
depends only on
q
and
k
but not on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
(as required).

Note that if
\eta+o>u
or
h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa)<0
or
o<\kappa

then by
Lemma

6.22
we have
p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)=0
and the lemma is immediately true. So we assume
\eta+o\leq u
,
h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa)\geq 0
and
o\geq\kappa

in the rest of this proof.
Let
h^{\prime}=h+\eta+o-(u+\kappa)
. By the inequalities above we have
0\leq h^{\prime}\leq h
.

We divide the analysis into five cases depending on the choice of
u
. (The cases differ first because
U_{C_{1},s}(u)
differs in behavior depending on whether
u\leq s
or not. Further differences arise in the analysis depending on the relationship between
u
and
h
, as also how close
u
is to
s
.)
The five cases are: (1)
1\leq u\leq h
, (2)
h<u\leq s
, (3)
s<u\leq 16s
, (4)
16s<u\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}n
, and (5)
\sqrt{\varepsilon}n<u\leq n
.

Case 1:
1\leq u\leq h
:

Expanding the definition of
U_{C_{1},s}(u)
,
U_{C_{2},s}(h^{\prime})
,
W_{C_{3},s}(h)
and invoking the upper bound on
p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)
from
Lemma

6.24
, we have that it suffices to prove that:

\displaystyle\left(C_{1}^{u/2}((sn)/u^{2})^{u/4}\right)\left(\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\right)\left(C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\right)\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\leq 4^{-u-2}\cdot C_{3}^{h/2}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{h/4}=4^{-u-2}\cdot U_{C_{3},s}(h)\,,

(6.27)

We multiply the LHS above by
4^{u+2}(h^{2}/sn)^{h/4}
and show it is upper bounded by
C_{3}^{h/2}
:

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle:=16(16C_{1})^{u/2}(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{4}^{u}C_{5}^{h}\cdot(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

(Using
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}\leq 1
,
h^{\prime}\leq h
,
h\leq 2^{h}
,
\kappa\leq o\leq u
,
C_{4}\geq 4\sqrt{C_{1}}\cdot C\cdot 2^{k}
,
C_{5}\geq 2\sqrt{C_{2}}
)

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{-(h-h^{\prime})/4}

(Using
(h/h^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}/2}\leq e^{h}
and
C_{6}\geq eC_{5}
)

\displaystyle=16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{-(u+\kappa-(\eta+o))/4}

\displaystyle=16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{3}/(s\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(su^{4}/(n\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(su^{4}/n^{3}h^{2})^{o/4}

\displaystyle=16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\varepsilon\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

\displaystyle=:S_{1}.

Thus far we have not used
u\leq h
. (We have only used
u\leq s
and this was to establish our goal as
Eq.

6.27
.) We now use
u\leq h
to analyze
S_{1}
.

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\varepsilon\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/\kappa^{4})^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Collecting
\varepsilon
terms)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Using
\eta\geq 0
,
o\geq\kappa
and
\varepsilon\leq 1
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(hn/(u^{2}))^{\kappa/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{o/2}

(Using
(u/\kappa)^{\kappa}\leq e^{u}
and
(u/\eta)^{\eta}\leq e^{u}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{(o-\kappa)/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u/h)^{(o-\kappa)/2}

(Using
u\leq n
and
o\geq\kappa
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{(u-\eta-o+\kappa)/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4}C_{6})^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{(u-\eta-o+\kappa)/2}\mbox{~~~(Using $u\leq h$ and $e^{2}C_{4}\geq C_{4}\geq 1$)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4}C_{6})^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}

(Using
h\geq u
and
u\geq u-o+\kappa-\eta
since
o\geq\kappa
and
\eta\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}C_{4}C_{6})^{h}\cdot e^{h}

(Using
(h/u)^{u/2}\leq e^{h/2}\leq e^{h}
.)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(\frac{1}{256}C_{3})^{h/2}\mbox{ ~~~(Using $C_{3}\geq(16e^{3}C_{4}C_{6})^{2}$)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{3}^{h/2}\mbox{ ~~~(Using $h\geq 1$).}

This yields
Eq.

6.27
in the range
u\in[h]
.

Case 2:
h<u\leq s
:

Here again our goal is to prove
Eq.

6.27
and we still have
L_{1}\leq S_{1}
. We proceed as follows:

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\varepsilon\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\varepsilon^{(u-h)/4}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Collecting
\varepsilon
terms and using
\eta+o-\kappa\geq u-h
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\varepsilon_{0}^{(u-h)/4}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Using
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and
h\leq u
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(hn/(u^{2}))^{\kappa/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{o/2}

(Using
(u/\kappa)^{\kappa}\leq e^{u}
and
(u/\eta)^{\eta}\leq e^{u}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{(o-\kappa)/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u/h)^{(o-\kappa)/2}

(Using
u\leq n
and
o\geq\kappa
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{(u-\eta-o+\kappa)/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\mbox{ ~~~~(Since $h\leq u$ and $u-\eta-o+\kappa\geq u-\eta-o\geq 0$)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\mbox{~~~(Using $\varepsilon_{0}\leq\frac{1}{(e^{2}C_{4})^{4}}$)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{3}^{h/2}\mbox{ ~~~(Using $C_{3}\geq(16C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{2}$, $h\geq 1$)}\,.

This concludes
Eq.

6.27
in Case 2.

Case 3:
s<u\leq 16s
:

The form for
U_{C_{1},s}(u)
now changes and forces a change in our goal. Using
U_{C_{1},s}(u)\leq C_{1}^{u/2}(n/u)^{u/4}
our new goal becomes:

\displaystyle\left(C_{1}^{u/2}(n/u)^{u/4}\right)\left(\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\right)\left(C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\right)\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\leq 4^{-u-2}\cdot C_{3}^{h/2}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{h/4}=4^{-u-2}\cdot U_{C_{3},s}(h)\,,

(6.28)

Again multiplying the LHS by
4^{u+2}(h^{2}/sn)^{h/4}
we get the quantity
L_{3}
below which we show to be upper bounded by
C_{3}^{h/2}
. We have:

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle:=16(16C_{1})^{u/2}(n/u)^{u/4}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

\displaystyle=(u/s)^{u/4}16(16C_{1})^{u/2}(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

\displaystyle=(u/s)^{u/4}L_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 16^{u/4}L_{1}

We now use the fact that the inequality
L_{1}\leq S_{1}
in Case 1, did not use
u\leq s
to conclude
L_{3}\leq 16^{u/4}L_{1}\leq 16^{u/4}S_{1}
. Similarly the inequality
S_{1}\leq 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}
from Case 2 also did not use
u\leq s
, and so we get
L_{3}\leq 16^{u/4}S_{1}\leq 16^{u/4}\cdot 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}
which we simplify below. We have:

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle\leq 16^{u/4}\cdot 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}

\displaystyle=16(2e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4}C_{4})^{u}(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}

\displaystyle\leq 16(C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{h}\mbox{~~~(Using $\varepsilon_{0}\leq\frac{1}{(2e^{2}C_{4})^{4}}$)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}^{h/2}\mbox{ ~~~(Using $C_{3}\geq(16C_{6}/\varepsilon_{0}^{1/4})^{2}$, $h\geq 1$)}\,.

This concludes Case 3.

Case 4:
16s<u\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}n
:

Here again it suffices to prove
Eq.

6.28
which is equivalent to proving

L_{3}\leq C_{3}^{h/2}
. We have

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle=16(16C_{1})^{u/2}(un/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

\displaystyle=16(16C_{1})^{u/2}(u/s)^{u/4}(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

\displaystyle\leq 16(16C_{1})^{u/2}\varepsilon^{-u/8}(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

(Using
s=\varepsilon n
and
u\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}n
yielding
u/s\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon=\varepsilon^{-1/2}
)

\displaystyle\leq 16(C_{4}/\varepsilon^{1/8})^{u}C_{5}^{h}\cdot(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

(Using
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}\leq 1
,
h^{\prime}\leq h
,
h\leq 2^{h}
,
\kappa\leq o\leq u
,
C_{4}\geq 4\sqrt{C_{1}}\cdot C\cdot 2^{k}
,
C_{5}\geq\max\{1,2C_{2}\}
)

\displaystyle\leq 16(C_{4}/\varepsilon^{1/8})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{-(h-h^{\prime})/4}

(Using
(h/h^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}/2}\leq e^{h})
and
C_{6}\geq eC_{5}
)

\displaystyle=16(C_{4}/\varepsilon^{1/8})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(sn/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{-(u+\kappa-(\eta+o))/4}

\displaystyle=16(C_{4}/\varepsilon^{1/8})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{3}/(s\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(su^{4}/(n\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(su^{4}/n^{3}h^{2})^{o/4}

\displaystyle=16(C_{4}/\varepsilon^{1/8})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\varepsilon\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

\displaystyle=16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot\varepsilon^{-u/8-\kappa/4+\eta/4+o/4}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/\kappa^{4})^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Collecting
\varepsilon
terms)

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\varepsilon^{u/8-h}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Using
\eta+o-\kappa\geq u-h
)

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\varepsilon^{u/16}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Using
u\geq 16s\geq 16h
in the form
h\leq u/16
to conclude
u/8-h\geq u/16
.)

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{4}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\varepsilon_{0}^{u/16}\cdot(h^{2}/u^{2})^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

(Using
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
)

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/16}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(hn/(u^{2}))^{\kappa/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{o/2}

(Using
(u/\kappa)^{\kappa}\leq e^{u}
and
(u/\eta)^{\eta}\leq e^{u}
)

\displaystyle=16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/16}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{(u+\kappa-\eta-o)/2}\cdot(u/n)^{(o-\kappa)/2}

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/16}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\cdot(h/u)^{(u+\kappa-\eta-o)/2}\mbox{~~~~~~~~ (Using $u\leq n$)}

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/16}C_{4})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\mbox{~~~~~~(Using $h\leq u$ and $u+\kappa-\eta-o\geq\kappa\geq 0$)}

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{6}^{h}\mbox{~~~~~~~~ (Using $\varepsilon_{0}\leq(e^{-2}/C_{4})^{16}$)}

\displaystyle\leq 16(C_{3}^{1/2}/16)^{h}\mbox{~~~~~~~~ (Using $C_{3}\geq 256C_{6}^{2}$)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}^{h/2}\mbox{~~~~~~~~ (Using $h\geq 1$).}

This establishes
Eq.

6.28
in Case 4.

Case 5:
\sqrt{\varepsilon}n<u\leq n
:

Here we use
U_{C_{1},s}(u)\leq(2q^{2}e^{2}n/u)^{u/2}
.
With this modification we need to prove:

\displaystyle\left((2q^{2}e^{2})^{u/2}(n/u)^{u/2}\right)\left(\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\right)\left(C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\right)\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\leq 4^{-u-2}\cdot C_{3}^{h/2}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{h/4}=4^{-u-2}\cdot U_{C_{3},s}(h)\,,

(6.29)

Multiplying the LHS by
4^{u+2}(h^{2}/sn)^{h/4}
we get the term
L_{5}
defined below which we wish to upper bound by
C_{3}^{h/2}
.

\displaystyle L_{5}

\displaystyle:=16\cdot 4^{u}\cdot(2q^{2}e^{2})^{u/2}(n/u)^{u/2}\cdot\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}C^{u}(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}(u/n)^{o}\cdot C_{2}^{h^{\prime}/2}(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{0}^{u}C_{5}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot(n/u)^{u/2}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{\prime 2})^{h^{\prime}/4}\cdot(h^{2}/(sn))^{h/4}

(Using
h^{\prime}\leq h\leq 2^{h}
,
\kappa\leq u
,
C_{0}\geq\sqrt{2}\cdot 4e\cdot q\cdot C\cdot 2^{k}
,
C_{5}\geq 2\sqrt{C_{2}}
)

\displaystyle\leq 16C_{0}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot(n/u)^{u/2}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{-(h-h^{\prime})/4}

(Using
(h/h^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}/2}\leq e^{h}
and
C_{6}\geq eC_{5}
)

\displaystyle=16C_{0}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot(n/u)^{u/2}\cdot(n/\kappa)^{\kappa}\cdot(u/\sqrt{n\eta})^{\eta}\cdot(u/n)^{o}\cdot(sn/h^{2})^{-(u+\kappa-(\eta+o))/4}

\displaystyle=16C_{0}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot(nh^{2}/(su^{2}))^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{3}/(s\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(su^{4}/(n\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(su^{4}/n^{3}h^{2})^{o/4}

\displaystyle=16C_{0}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot(h^{2}/(\varepsilon u^{2}))^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\varepsilon\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(\varepsilon u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

\displaystyle=16C_{0}^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-u-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h^{2}/(u^{2}))^{u/4}\cdot(h^{2}n^{2}/(\kappa^{4}))^{\kappa/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(\eta^{2}h^{2}))^{\eta/4}\cdot(u^{4}/(n^{2}h^{2}))^{o/4}

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}C_{0})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-u-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(hn/(u^{2}))^{\kappa/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{o/2}

(Using
(u/\kappa)^{\kappa}\leq e^{u}
and
(u/\eta)^{\eta}\leq e^{u}
)

\displaystyle=16(e^{2}C_{0})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-u-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u^{2}/(nh))^{(o-\kappa)/2}

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}C_{0})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(o+\eta)/k}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-u-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h/u)^{u/2}\cdot(u/h)^{\eta/2}\cdot(u/h)^{(o-\kappa)/2}

(Using
u\leq n
and
o\geq\kappa
)

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}C_{0})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha^{(u-h)/k}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-u-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h/u)^{(u-\eta-o+\kappa)/2}

(Using
\alpha\leq 1
and
u-h\leq\eta+o
)

\displaystyle\leq 16(e^{2}C_{0})^{u}C_{6}^{h}\alpha_{0}^{(u-h)/k}\cdot\varepsilon^{(-u-\kappa+\eta+o)/4}\cdot(h/u)^{(u-\eta-o+\kappa)/2}

(Using
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and
u-h\geq 0
)

\displaystyle=16(\alpha_{0}^{1/k}e^{2}C_{0})^{u}(C_{6}/\alpha_{0}^{1/k})^{h}(h^{2}/(\varepsilon u^{2}))^{(u-\eta-o+\kappa)/4}

\displaystyle\leq 16(\alpha_{0}^{1/k}e^{2}C_{0})^{u}(C_{6}/\alpha_{0}^{1/k})^{h}

(Using
h\leq s=\varepsilon n
and
u\geq\sqrt{\varepsilon}n
to conclude
h^{2}/(\varepsilon u^{2})\leq 1
. Also using
u-\eta-o+\kappa\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\leq 16(C_{6}/\alpha_{0}^{1/k})^{h}~~~\mbox{(Using $\alpha_{0}\leq 1/(e^{2}C_{0})^{k}$)}

\displaystyle\leq 16(C_{3}^{1/2}/16)^{h}~~~\mbox{(Using $C_{3}\geq(16C_{6}/\alpha_{0}^{1/k})^{2}$)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}^{h/2}~~~\mbox{(Using $h\geq 1$).}\,

This concludes the analysis of Case 5 and proves the lemma.
∎

Step 3: Proof of
Lemma

6.18
.

We are now ready to combine the ingredients from the previous steps to prove
Lemma

6.18
.

Proof of
6.18
.

Let
\alpha_{0}
be the as given by
Lemma

6.26
. Let

\varepsilon_{0}
and
C_{3}
be the parameters given by
Lemma

6.26
for
C_{1}=C
and
C_{2}=C_{0}
.
We prove the lemma for
C^{\prime\prime}=C_{3}
and
\tau_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}
.

Let
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and
m=\alpha n
. For every
s\leq\varepsilon_{0}n
, we prove that the LHS in the lemma statement is upper bounded by
W_{C_{3},s}(h)
for every
h\in[s]
.
In the following, for every matching
M
of size
m
, we fix a
B^{\prime}=B^{\prime}(M)
that is
(M,C_{0},s)
-reduced. (The inequalities hold for every such fixing.)
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\ \sum_{u=0}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
|\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})|=u\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{M}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
\|\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{0}=h\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B^{\prime}|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\ \sum_{u=0}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}\\
|\textsf{supp}(\mathbf{u})|=u\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{n}}{|B|}\left|\widehat{\mathbf{1}_{B}}(\mathbf{u})\right|\cdot\sum_{o,\eta,\kappa}p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C_{0},s}(h+o+\eta-(u+\kappa))

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq\ \sum_{u=0}^{n}\sum_{o,\eta,\kappa}U_{C,s}(u)\cdot p_{q,\alpha}(n,u,o,\eta,\kappa)\cdot h\cdot 2^{k\kappa}\cdot U_{C_{0},s}(h+o+\eta-(u+\kappa))

(6.31)

\displaystyle\leq\ \sum_{u=0}^{n}\sum_{o,\eta,\kappa}4^{-u-2}W_{C_{3},s}(h)

(6.32)

\displaystyle\leq\ \sum_{u=0}^{n}(u+1)^{3}\cdot 4^{-u-2}\cdot W_{C_{3},s}

\displaystyle\leq\ W_{C_{3},s}(h),

where (
6.30
) follows from
6.22
and the fact that
B^{\prime}
is
(M,C_{0},s)
-reduced, (
6.31
) follows from the fact that
B
is
(C,s)
-bounded, and (
6.32
) follows from Lemma
6.26
for
C_{3}
as defined above. This proves the lemma.
∎
